- **writing-for-agents-zh**: 给 Agent 写文档、写 SKILL.md、写 AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md、写规则文件、让 Agent 每次跑都稳定。当用户说「怎么写一个好的 SKILL.md」「AGENTS.md 该写什么」「技能描述怎么写才会被触发」「文档太长 Agent 不看」「同一个技能每次表现不一样」「什么时候该拆成两个技能」时使用。核心概念：上下文指针（描述与规则行的措辞决定 Agent 何时去读）；两种负担（上下文负担 vs 认知负担）；信息层级（文件内步骤 &gt; 文件内参考 &gt; 披露到外部的参考）与渐进披露、同处放置、蔓延；每步的完成标准（清晰度与要求度、过早完成、后续步骤的拉力）；何时拆分（按序列、按调用方式）；引导词（用模型预训练里已有的紧凑概念锚定行为，正面表述优于禁止）；修剪（单一事实来源、环境即缓存、相关性、空操作句）。技能专属：模型调用 vs 用户调用、路由技能。也覆盖「改得更容易被正确触发」「别写成一本手册」「老是漏掉后面的步骤」这类说法。改编自 Matt Pocock 的 writing-for-agents（MIT）。 ([po-et/workbuddy-skills](https://github.com/po-et/workbuddy-skills/tree/HEAD/ported/skills/writing-for-agents-zh) · ★4)
- **writing-for-ai**: Write instructions an agent follows the same way every run, at the lowest load: SKILL.md files, subagent definitions and charters, the prompts a skill gives its subagents, CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md, and slash commands. Use when creating, restructuring, tightening, or reviewing one. Not for one-line fixes, prompts written on the fly, or writing for people. ([alisterpineda/ai](https://github.com/alisterpineda/ai/tree/HEAD/plugins/core/skills/writing-for-ai))

- **writing-for-audiences**: Use when writing prose a specific person or audience will read, act on, or hear — a report, a deck or speech, teaching material, an explanation, a message — and when a reader signals they did not understand ("what is this number of?", "explain it in simpler terms", "I don't get what you mean"). ([ViktorSoroka07/claude-delivery-skills](https://github.com/ViktorSoroka07/claude-delivery-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/writing-for-audiences))
- **writing-for-computer-science**: Review, revise, and draft scientific and technical writing (computing, mathematics, theses, papers, reports) to the standards of Justin Zobel's "Writing for Computer Science". ([inacior/writing-for-computer-science](https://github.com/inacior/writing-for-computer-science/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★1)

- **writing-for-engineers**: Write or revise technical documentation a human engineer reads: architecture and reference docs, runbooks, decision records, write-ups and reports. ([mkucharek/skills](https://github.com/mkucharek/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/writing/skills/writing-for-engineers))

- **writing-humans**: Create and revise texts intended for people: documents, theoretical or technical material, explanations, reports, emails, summaries, and presentations. Apply proactively during relevant writing tasks, from a request or source material, without requiring an explicit invocation or an existing draft. Write clearly, naturally, precisely, and coherently while preserving meaning, voice, and depth. For instructions and operational content agents use to execute or coordinate work, use writing-agents. ([nushey/sdd-flow](https://github.com/nushey/sdd-flow/tree/HEAD/skills/writing-humans) · ★1)

- **writing-i18n**: Organize Rails I18n — YAML structure, lazy lookup in views, absolute keys in helpers and controllers, interpolation and pluralization, datetime/number formats, and Active Record error messages. DHH/37signals style: boring keys, framework-first, no string concatenation in templates. Use when adding or changing locales, extracting user-facing copy from ERB, flash messages, or validation messages; not for translation vendor tooling or SPA i18n-js. - **writing-idempotent-transformations**: Write data transformations and loads that produce the same result no matter how many times they run — using MERGE/upsert, deterministic partition overwrites, deduplication, and stable keys. Use when a retry could duplicate data, a job is not safe to re-run, a pipeline needs exactly-once effects, or loads must be backfill-safe. - **writing-idiomatic-python**: Use when writing or reviewing Python (3.10+) — modern idioms for typing, data modeling, control flow, and standard-library use, plus the anti-patterns to avoid (mutable default args, bare except, manual index loops, string-built paths). ([meaganewaller/rosetta](https://github.com/meaganewaller/rosetta/tree/HEAD/plugins/python-idioms/skills/writing-idiomatic-python) · ★1)
- **writing-idiomatic-ruby**: Use when writing or reviewing Ruby — Enumerable-driven, expression-oriented idioms (blocks, guard clauses, safe navigation, keyword arguments, predicate methods) and the manual loops, nil checks, and temporary variables they replace. ([meaganewaller/rosetta](https://github.com/meaganewaller/rosetta/tree/HEAD/plugins/ruby-idioms/skills/writing-idiomatic-ruby) · ★1)
- **writing-idiomatic-rust**: Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring Rust code — API design, naming, doc comments, error types, tests, ownership — before finishing or shipping any Rust change. Reference of judgment-call rules from Google, Microsoft Pragmatic, Rust API Guidelines, Effective Rust, ANSSI, Tiger Style, and Canonical that clippy cannot check. - **writing-idiomatic-typescript**: Use when writing or reviewing TypeScript — getting value from the type system (unknown over any, discriminated unions, narrowing, satisfies, readonly) and avoiding the escape hatches (any, unchecked as casts, non-null !) that silently defeat it. ([meaganewaller/rosetta](https://github.com/meaganewaller/rosetta/tree/HEAD/plugins/typescript-idioms/skills/writing-idiomatic-typescript) · ★1)
- **writing-idioms**: Flag misused, misquoted, mixed, or non-idiomatic idioms and proverbs in a draft and propose the canonical form as a TODO PROPOSAL.
